A BILL FOR AN ACT
101 CONCERNING PATHWAYS FOR INDIVIDUALS WITH MENTAL HEALTH
102 DISORDERS, AND, IN CONNECTION THEREWITH, MAKING AN
103 APPROPRIATION.
Bill Summary
(Note: This summary applies to this bill as introduced and does
not reflect any amendments that may be subsequently adopted. If this bill
passes third reading in the house of introduction, a bill summary that
applies to the reengrossed version of this bill will be available at
http://leg.colorado.gov.)
Under current law, if a defendant is found incompetent to proceed
and the defendant will not be restored to competency in the foreseeable
future, the court is required to dismiss charges against the defendant and
the defendant, in limited circumstances, may be referred for certification
for short-term treatment through a civil court process. The bill maintains

SENATE SPONSORSHIP
Amabile and Simpson, Baisley, Ball, Bright, Carson, Catlin, Coleman, Gonzales J., Jodeh,
Kipp, Kirkmeyer, Kolker, Marchman, Pelton B., Pelton R., Rich, Wallace, Zamora Wilson
HOUSE SPONSORSHIP
Caldwell and McCluskie,
Shading denotes HOUSE amendment. Double underlining denotes SENATE amendment.
Capital letters or bold & italic numbers indicate new material to be added to existing law.
Dashes through the words or numbers indicate deletions from existing law.

the requirement for the court to dismiss the charges against a defendant
if the defendant's highest charged offense is certain misdemeanor
offenses and maintains certification for short-term treatment as an option
for the defendant. However, the bill authorizes the district attorney or
county attorney (prosecution) to notify the court that the prosecution
seeks civil commitment or an enhanced protective placement of a
defendant if the prosecution can prove by clear and convincing evidence
that the defendant:
! Has a mental disability or developmental disability;
! Committed an act of homicide, crime of violence, or felony
that constitutes unlawful sexual behavior; and
! Poses a substantial risk of serious harm to others.
If the prosecution seeks civil commitment or an enhanced
protective placement, the court is required to stay the order dismissing the
defendant's case, set a trial within 91 days after the date the written notice
was filed, and order the office of forensic and mental health (OCFMH)
in the department of human services (CDHS) to identify an appropriate
provider and placement for the defendant in the event a civil commitment
or enhanced protective placement is granted. If the court finds the
prosecution has not met its burden, the court is required to deny the
prosecution's request to civilly commit the defendant or order an
enhanced protective placement of the defendant. If the court finds the
prosecution has met its burden, the court is required to make a finding of
the defendant's primary diagnosis that constitutes the mental disability or
developmental disability. The court shall order the defendant civilly
committed to CDHS unless the defendant's primary diagnosis is an
intellectual and developmental disability (IDD) or a neurocognitive
disorder, in which case, the court shall order an enhanced protective
placement of the defendant to the department of health care policy and
financing (HCPF).
After ordering a civil commitment or enhanced protective
placement, the court has 70 days to review and approve a placement for
the defendant, place the defendant, transfer jurisdiction to the appropriate
civil court, and dismiss the defendant's criminal case. If an appropriate
placement is not identified by OCFMH within the specified time frames,
the court is required to place the defendant in the physical care and
custody of a state hospital; except that, if the defendant's primary
diagnosis includes an IDD or a neurocognitive disorder, the court shall
not place the defendant in a state hospital unless placement in a regional
center or skilled nursing facility is unavailable or inappropriate.
Upon receiving jurisdiction of a civil commitment or enhanced
protective placement, the bill requires the civil court to supervise the civil
commitment or enhanced protective placement by notifying the county
attorney, appointing an attorney to represent the respondent, and setting
a review hearing. At the hearing, the respondent has the right to request
-2- 149

modification of the terms of the civil commitment or enhanced protective
placement and the right to periodic review, including whether the
respondent qualifies for termination of the civil commitment or enhanced
protective placement. The court is required to ensure the respondent is
placed in the least-restrictive setting adequate to protect the victims and
community. The court shall not modify the civil commitment or enhanced
protective placement and place the respondent into a state hospital unless
the court has exhausted all reasonable attempts to find an alternative and
no other less-restrictive placements are adequate to protect the victims
and the community. The provider charged with the physical care and
custody of the respondent is required to submit a report to the court and
the parties annually by the date the respondent was civilly committed or
ordered into enhanced protective placement unless a substantially similar
examination was ordered by the court within the previous 12 months.
The bill requires the court to terminate the respondent's civil
commitment or enhanced protective placement if the respondent no
longer poses a substantial risk of serious harm to others or the respondent
does not have the applicable disorder or disability that is likely to cause
the respondent to be a danger to the respondent's self or a danger to others
and the respondent has demonstrated sufficient capacity and willingness
to conform their conduct to the requirements of the law. If the respondent
does not meet the criteria for termination, the respondent is not entitled
to another termination trial within one year after the conclusion of the
previous trial.
The court shall convert a civil commitment to an enhanced
protective placement if the respondent does not meet the criteria for
termination but the respondent has a mental health disorder that is an IDD
or a neurocognitive disorder, without having any other mental health
disorder that is not an IDD or a neurocognitive disorder, and that
substantially contributes to whether the respondent is a danger to the
respondent's self or a danger to others, or is gravely disabled. If the
defendant does not meet the criteria for termination and has co-occurring
mental health disorders that include an IDD or a neurocognitive disorder,
the court may, upon the recommendation of OCFMH, convert the civil
commitment to an enhanced protective placement.
The court shall convert an enhanced protective placement to a civil
commitment if the respondent does not meet the criteria for termination
and the respondent no longer has an IDD or a neurocognitive disorder that
substantially contributes to whether the respondent is a danger to the
respondent's self or a danger to others, or is gravely disabled. If the
defendant does not meet the criteria for termination and has co-occurring
mental health disorders that do not include an IDD or a neurocognitive
disorder, the court may, upon the recommendation of OCFMH, convert
the enhanced protective placement to a civil commitment.
Under current law, an emergency mental health hold (M1 hold)
-3- 149

may be initiated against a person for not more than 72 hours if the person
appears to have a mental health disorder and as a result of the mental
health disorder, appears to be a danger to the person's self or others, or
appears to be gravely disabled. A person detained for an M1 hold and
transported to an emergency medical services facility or facility
designated by the commissioner (facility) of the behavioral health
administration (BHA) and is required to receive an evaluation as soon as
possible after the person presents to the facility. Rather than being
transported to a facility, the bill authorizes a person who has an M1 hold
initiated against them while in confinement to receive an evaluation at the
person's place of confinement. If the person is released from confinement,
the person responsible for the confinement is required to coordinate with
the BHA to transfer the person to a facility.
Under current law, if a person under an M1 hold meets the criteria
for certification for short-term treatment, the person may be certified for
not more than 3 months. Rather than requiring an M1 hold as a
prerequisite to short-term certification, the bill authorizes a person to be
certified if the person:
! Has a persistent mental health disorder; or
! Has a mental health disorder and, as a result of the mental
health disorder, the person is unwilling or unable to comply
with voluntary treatment, or reasonable grounds exist to
believe that the person will not remain in a voluntary
treatment program and is a danger to the person's self, a
danger to others, or gravely disabled.
A person may not be certified for short-term treatment or
long-term care and treatment if the person has an IDD or neurocognitive
disorder without any other mental health disorder that is not an IDD or
neurocognitive disorder and that substantially contributes to whether the
respondent is a danger to the respondent's self or a danger to others, or is
gravely disabled. If a respondent is certified, the court shall order
OCFMH to provide care coordination and make diligent efforts to find a
provider for the respondent that is willing to hold the certification. The
respondent may be certified for long-term care and treatment if the
respondent continues to meet the criteria and standards for certification
for short-term treatment. The court shall terminate the certification if the
respondent no longer meets the criteria for certification.
A court may order the short-term or long-term protective
placement of a person who:
! Has a neurocognitive disorder;
! Is unwilling or unable to comply with voluntary treatment,
or reasonable grounds exist to believe that the person will
not remain in a voluntary treatment program; and
! Is a danger to the person's self, a danger to others, or
gravely disabled.
-4- 149

The bill aligns the provisions for short-term and long-term
protective placement with certifications for short-term treatment and
long-term care with treatment.
HCPF, in collaboration with the BHA, is responsible for finding
an appropriate provider and placement for a person subject to a short-term
or long-term protective placement. A protective placement may be
terminated upon the signature of the treating medical professional and the
medical director of the facility if, after a reasonable observation and
treatment period, the treating medical professional determines the
respondent no longer meets the criteria for protective placement.
The bill makes conforming amendments.

9 16-8.5-102. Competency to proceed - how and when raised.
10 (1) While a defendant is incompetent to proceed, the defendant
11 must not be tried or sentenced, nor shall the court consider or decide
12 pretrial matters that are not susceptible of fair determination without the
13 personal participation of the defendant. However, a determination that a
14 defendant is incompetent to proceed does not preclude the furtherance of
15 the ADDITIONAL proceedings by the court to consider and decide matters,
16 including a preliminary hearing and motions, that are susceptible of fair
17 determination prior to trial and without the personal participation of the
18 defendant. Those proceedings may be later reopened if, in the discretion
19 of the court, substantial new evidence is discovered after and as a result
20 of the defendant's restoration to competency.
21 (2) The question of a defendant's competency to proceed must be
22 raised in only one of the following manners:
23 (a) If the judge has reason to believe that the defendant is
24 incompetent to proceed, the judge shall suspend the proceeding and
25 determine the competency or incompetency of the defendant pursuant to
26 section 16-8.5-103;
27 (b) If either the defense or the prosecution has reason to believe
-11- 149

1 that the defendant is incompetent to proceed, either party may file a
2 motion in advance of the commencement of the particular proceeding. A
3 motion to determine competency shall MUST be in writing and contain a
4 certificate of counsel stating that the motion is based on a good faith
5 doubt that the defendant is competent to proceed. The motion must set
6 forth the specific facts that have formed the basis for the motion. The
7 court must SHALL seal the motion. If the motion is made by the
8 prosecution, the prosecution shall provide the defense a copy of the
9 motion. If the motion is made by the defense, the defense shall provide
10 the prosecution notice of the filing of the motion at the time of filing, and
11 if the defense requests a hearing, the defense shall provide the motion to
12 the prosecution at the time the hearing is requested. The motion may be
13 filed after the commencement of the proceeding if, for good cause shown,
14 the defendant's mental disability or developmental disability was not
15 known or apparent before the commencement of the proceeding.
16 (c) Repealed.
17 (d) (c) By the public defender liaison, as described in section
18 21-1-104 (6), or an attorney representing the offender in a parole
19 proceeding.

14 16-8.5-103. Determination of competency to proceed.
15 (1) (a) Whenever the question of a defendant's competency to
16 proceed is raised, by either party or on the court's own motion, the court
17 may make a preliminary finding of competency or incompetency to
18 proceed, which is a final determination unless a party to the case objects
19 within seven days after the court's preliminary finding.
20 (b) On or before the date when a court orders that a defendant be
21 evaluated for competency, a bridges court liaison for the district hired or
22 contracted pursuant to article 95 of title 13 may be assigned to the
23 defendant.
24 (2) If either party objects to the court's preliminary finding, or if
25 the court determines that it has insufficient information to make a
26 preliminary finding, the court shall order that the defendant be evaluated
27 for competency by the department CDHS and that the department CDHS
-13- 149

1 prepare a court-ordered report.
2 (3) Within fourteen days after receipt of the court-ordered report,
3 either party may request a hearing or a second evaluation.
4 (4) If a party requests a second evaluation, any pending requests
5 for a hearing must be continued until the receipt of the second evaluation
6 report. The report of the expert conducting the second evaluation must be
7 completed and filed with the court within thirty-five days after the court
8 order allowing the second evaluation, unless the time period is extended
9 by the court for good cause. The court shall provide the second evaluation
10 to the parties and the department. The department shall use the second
11 evaluation to ensure that the department complies with its responsibilities,
12 including reviewing and summarizing prior competency opinions as
13 required by section 16-8.5-105 (5)(f). If the second evaluation is

1 CDHS for A COMPETENCY evaluation, or for restoration treatment

10 such LEGAL representation exists, and to the defendant directly if the
11 defendant does not have legal representation. A physician shall assess and
12 document the defendant's mental status prior to the administration of
13 medication.
14 (2) A petition for involuntary treatment must be heard in The
15 court of the jurisdiction where the defendant is located The department
16 SHALL HEAR A PETITION FOR INVOLUNTARY TREATMENT. CDHS shall
17 promptly deliver a copy of the order granting or denying the petition to
18 the court that committed the defendant to the custody of the department
19 CDHS, the prosecuting attorney, and the defendant's legal representation
20 in the criminal case, if such LEGAL representation exists, and to the
21 defendant directly if the defendant does not have legal representation.
22 (3) If the committing court elects to transfer venue for medication
23 hearings to the court of the jurisdiction where the defendant is located IF
24 A HEARING FOR ADMINISTRATION OF INVOLUNTARY MEDICATION IS HEARD
25 IN A DIFFERENT COUNTY THAN THE COUNTY WHERE THE COMMITTING
26 COURT IS LOCATED, the committing county shall reimburse the county
27 where the proceeding is heard for the reasonable costs incurred in
-30- 149

1 conducting the proceeding. Alternatively, the district attorney OR COUNTY
2 ATTORNEY for the committing county or in any county or any city and

16 16-8.5-107. [Formerly 16-8.5-108 (1)] Use of defendant's
17 statements.
18 (1) (a) Except as otherwise provided in this subsection (1)
19 SECTION, evidence acquired directly or indirectly for the first time from
20 a communication derived from the defendant's mental processes during
21 the course of a competency evaluation or involuntary medication
22 proceeding is not admissible against the defendant on the issues raised by
23 a plea of not guilty, or, if the offense occurred before July 1, 1995, a plea
24 of not guilty by reason of impaired mental condition. Such THE evidence
25 may be admissible at trial to rebut evidence introduced by the defendant
26 of the defendant's mental condition to show incapacity of the defendant
27 THE DEFENDANT'S INCAPACITY to form a culpable mental state; and, in
-31- 149

1 such THAT case, the evidence may only be considered by the trier of fact
2 as bearing upon the question of capacity to form a culpable mental state,
3 and the jury shall be so instructed at the request of either party.
4 (b) (2) Evidence acquired directly or indirectly for the first time
5 from a communication derived from the defendant's mental processes
6 during the course of a competency evaluation or involuntary medication
7 proceeding is admissible at any sentencing hearing held pursuant to
8 section 18-1.3-1201 for an offense charged prior to July 1, 2020, or
9 pursuant to section 18-1.3-1302 for an offense charged prior to July 1,
10 2020, or pursuant to section 18-1.4-102 only to prove the existence or
11 absence of any mitigating factor.
12 (c) (3) If the defendant testifies on the defendant's own behalf
13 upon the trial of the issues raised by the plea of not guilty or, for offenses
14 that occurred before July 1, 1995, a plea of not guilty by reason of
15 impaired mental condition, or at a sentencing hearing held pursuant to
16 section 18-1.3-1201 for an offense charged prior to July 1, 2020, or
17 pursuant to section 18-1.3-1302 for an offense charged prior to July 1,
18 2020, or pursuant to section 18-1.4-102, this section does not bar any
19 evidence used to impeach or rebut the defendant's testimony.
20 16-8.5-108. General hearing procedures and evidence.
21 (1) (a) A PARTY MUST REQUEST A COMPETENCY HEARING,
22 RESTORABILITY HEARING, OR RESTORATION HEARING WITHIN FOURTEEN
23 DAYS AFTER RECEIVING THE INITIAL OR UPDATED COURT-ORDERED
24 COMPETENCY EVALUATION REPORT; EXCEPT THAT, IF A PARTY REQUESTS
25 A SECOND EVALUATION PURSUANT TO SECTION 16-8.5-111, A PARTY MUST
26 REQUEST THE COMPETENCY HEARING, RESTORABILITY HEARING, OR
27 RESTORATION HEARING WITHIN FOURTEEN DAYS AFTER RECEIVING THE
-32- 149

1 SECOND EVALUATION REPORT.
2 (b) (I) A REQUEST FOR A COMPETENCY HEARING IS GOVERNED BY
3 SECTION 16-8.5-109.
4 (II) A REQUEST FOR A RESTORABILITY HEARING IS GOVERNED BY
5 SECTION 16-8.5-113.
6 (III) A REQUEST FOR A RESTORATION HEARING IS GOVERNED BY
7 SECTION 16-8.5-114.
8 (IV) A REQUEST FOR A COMBINED RESTORABILITY AND
9 RESTORATION HEARING IS GOVERNED BY SECTIONS 16-8.5-113 AND
10 16-8.5-114.
11 (c) THE COURT SHALL GIVE THE NONMOVING PARTY AN
12 OPPORTUNITY TO OBJECT AND SHALL GRANT OR DENY THE REQUEST FOR
13 A COMPETENCY HEARING, RESTORABILITY HEARING, OR RESTORATION
14 HEARING IN ACCORDANCE WITH THE APPLICABLE GOVERNING STATUTES
15 WITHIN FOURTEEN DAYS AFTER THE REQUEST.
16 (d) THE COMPETENCY HEARING, RESTORABILITY HEARING, OR
17 RESTORATION HEARING MUST BE HELD WITHIN THIRTY-FIVE DAYS AFTER
18 THE COURT'S ORDER GRANTING THE REQUEST, UNLESS THE TIME IS
19 EXTENDED BY THE COURT AFTER A FINDING OF GOOD CAUSE.
20 (2) (a) [Formerly 16-8.5-110] In any AT A COMPETENCY hearing,
21 at which the competency of the defendant is an issue RESTORABILITY
22 HEARING, OR RESTORATION HEARING, witnesses not specially trained in
23 psychiatry or psychology and not testifying as expert witnesses may
24 testify as to the witness's THEIR observation of the defendant's actions and
25 conduct and as to conversations that the witness THEY had with the
26 defendant bearing upon the defendant's mental condition. Any such
27 witnesses, as part of the witness's THEIR testimony, must be permitted to
-33- 149

1 give opinions or conclusions concerning the competency of the defendant.

-36- 149

1 subsection (3) of this section or section 16-8.5-116.5 (7) and, upon

1 (6) to (15) Repealed.
2 16-8.5-113. Restorability hearing - burdens of proof -
3 determination - dismissal.
4 (1) (a) THE COURT MAY, UPON MOTION OF A PARTY AND UPON A
5 SHOWING OF GOOD CAUSE, SET A RESTORABILITY HEARING WITHIN THE
6 TIME FRAME SET FORTH IN SECTION 16-8.5-108 (1)(d).
7 (b) IF THE FINAL DETERMINATION MADE PURSUANT TO SECTION
8 16-8.5-103 IS THAT THE DEFENDANT IS INCOMPETENT TO PROCEED AND A
9 COMPETENCY EVALUATOR OPINES THAT THE DEFENDANT IS
10 UNRESTORABLE, AND EITHER A RESTORABILITY HEARING HAS NOT BEEN
11 HELD OR ONE HUNDRED EIGHTY-TWO DAYS HAVE PASSED SINCE THE
12 DEFENDANT BEGAN RECEIVING RESTORATION SERVICES AFTER A FINDING
13 OF RESTORABILITY, THE COURT SHALL, UPON MOTION OF A PARTY, SET A
14 RESTORABILITY HEARING WITHIN THE TIME FRAME SET FORTH IN SECTION
15 16-8.5-108 (1)(d). A RESTORABILITY HEARING MAY BE COMBINED WITH A
16 RESTORATION HEARING, IF APPROPRIATE.
17 (c) IF THE COURT RECEIVES THE COMPETENCY EVALUATOR'S
18 OPINION THAT THE DEFENDANT IS UNRESTORABLE PRIOR TO ENTERING AN
19 INITIAL ORDER FOR RESTORATION SERVICES, THE COURT SHALL SET A
20 RESTORABILITY HEARING WITHIN THIRTY-FIVE DAYS AFTER RECEIVING THE
21 OPINION UNLESS THE TIME IS EXTENDED BY THE COURT AFTER A FINDING
22 OF GOOD CAUSE.
23 (2) AT ANY RESTORABILITY HEARING CONDUCTED PURSUANT TO
24 THIS SECTION:
25 (a) THE DEFENDANT HAS THE BURDEN OF PROVING BY A
26 PREPONDERANCE OF THE EVIDENCE THAT THE DEFENDANT IS
27 UNRESTORABLE IF ANY CHARGE IN ANY OF THE DEFENDANT'S PENDING
-51- 149

1 CRIMINAL CASES IN THE STATE OF COLORADO INCLUDE A CRIME SUBJECT
2 TO THE "VICTIM RIGHTS ACT", SECTION 24-4.1-302 (1); UNLAWFUL
3 SEXUAL CONTACT, AS DESCRIBED IN SECTION 18-3-404; OR INDECENT
4 EXPOSURE, AS DESCRIBED IN SECTION 18-7-302; AND
5 (b) THE PROSECUTION HAS THE BURDEN OF PROVING BY A
6 PREPONDERANCE OF THE EVIDENCE THAT THE DEFENDANT IS RESTORABLE
7 IF THE DEFENDANT DOES NOT HAVE A PENDING CRIMINAL CASE IN THE
8 STATE OF COLORADO THAT INCLUDES A CHARGE OF A CRIME SUBJECT TO
9 THE "VICTIM RIGHTS ACT", SECTION 24-4.1-302 (1); UNLAWFUL SEXUAL
10 CONTACT, AS DESCRIBED IN SECTION 18-3-404; OR INDECENT EXPOSURE,
11 AS DESCRIBED IN SECTION 18-7-302.
12 (3) WHEN DETERMINING WHETHER THE DEFENDANT IS
13 RESTORABLE OR UNRESTORABLE, THE COURT SHALL CONSIDER ALL
14 RELEVANT INFORMATION, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO:
15 (a) ANY DIAGNOSED MENTAL DISORDER OR DEVELOPMENTAL
16 DISABILITY GIVING RISE TO THE DEFENDANT'S INCOMPETENCY, INCLUDING
17 AN INTELLECTUAL AND DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY, AS DEFINED IN
18 SECTION 25.5-10-202, OR NEUROCOGNITIVE DISORDER, AS DEFINED IN
19 SECTION 25.5-10-501, AND WHETHER THE DIAGNOSED MENTAL DISORDER
20 OR DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY CAN BE TREATED, MITIGATED, OR
21 MANAGED IN A WAY THAT WOULD ALLOW THE DEFENDANT TO PROGRESS
22 TOWARD BECOMING COMPETENT TO PROCEED;
23 (b) THE NATURE AND SEVERITY OF THE DEFENDANT'S
24 INCOMPETENCY AND WHETHER THE DEFENDANT'S LEVEL OF COMPETENCY
25 CAN BE IMPROVED THROUGH ANY SERVICES THE COURT MAY LAWFULLY
26 ORDER, INCLUDING SERVICES THAT ARE IN ADDITION TO RESTORATION
27 SERVICES;
-52- 149

1 (c) THE EXPERIENCES, OBSERVATIONS, AND OPINIONS OF
2 QUALIFIED EXPERTS, INCLUDING MEDICAL PROFESSIONALS, TREATMENT
3 PROVIDERS, AND RESTORATION SPECIALISTS;
4 (d) THE EXPERIENCES, OBSERVATIONS, AND OPINIONS OF LAY
5 PERSONS WHO ARE FAMILIAR WITH THE DEFENDANT, INCLUDING FAMILY
6 MEMBERS, FRIENDS, ASSOCIATES, AND ANY OTHER INDIVIDUAL WITH
7 WHOM THE DEFENDANT HAS HAD SIGNIFICANT INTERACTIONS;
8 (e) THE DEFENDANT'S MEDICAL HISTORY, CRIMINAL HISTORY,
9 COMPETENCY AND RESTORATION HISTORY, AND CIVIL COMMITMENT
10 HISTORY;
11 (f) THE FACTS AND CONTEXT OF CURRENT AND PAST CHARGES
12 AGAINST THE DEFENDANT AS EVIDENCED BY POLICE REPORTS, VIDEO OR
13 AUDIO RECORDINGS, PHYSICAL EVIDENCE, WITNESS OR VICTIM
14 STATEMENTS, AND ANY OTHER RELIABLE SOURCES;
15 (g) ANY RELEVANT STATEMENTS MADE BY THE DEFENDANT
16 DURING THE RESTORATION PROCESS; OR
17 (h) THE DEFENDANT'S LEVEL OF EFFORT AND ENGAGEMENT,
18 INCLUDING ANY VOLITIONAL LACK OF COOPERATION OR UNWILLINGNESS
19 TO PARTICIPATE.
20 (4) (a) AT THE CONCLUSION OF A RESTORABILITY HEARING SET
21 PURSUANT TO THIS SECTION:
22 (I) IF THE COURT FINDS THAT THE DEFENDANT HAS NOT MET THE
23 BURDEN OF PROVING THE DEFENDANT IS UNRESTORABLE PURSUANT TO
24 SUBSECTION (2)(a) OF THIS SECTION, THE COURT SHALL FIND THE
25 DEFENDANT RESTORABLE AND ORDER APPROPRIATE RESTORATION
26 SERVICES AND SET A REVIEW HEARING PURSUANT TO SECTION 16-8.5-112.
27 (II) IF THE COURT FINDS THAT THE DEFENDANT HAS MET THE
-53- 149

1 BURDEN OF PROVING THEY ARE UNRESTORABLE PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION
2 (2)(a) OF THIS SECTION, THE COURT SHALL FIND THE DEFENDANT
3 UNRESTORABLE.
4 (III) IF THE COURT FINDS THAT THE PROSECUTION HAS NOT MET
5 THE BURDEN OF PROVING THE DEFENDANT IS RESTORABLE PURSUANT TO
6 SUBSECTION (2)(b) OF THIS SECTION, THE COURT SHALL FIND THE
7 DEFENDANT UNRESTORABLE.
8 (IV) IF THE COURT FINDS THAT THE PROSECUTION HAS MET THE
9 BURDEN OF PROVING THE DEFENDANT IS RESTORABLE PURSUANT TO
10 SUBSECTION (2)(b) OF THIS SECTION, THE COURT SHALL FIND THE
11 DEFENDANT RESTORABLE AND ORDER APPROPRIATE RESTORATION
12 SERVICES AND SET A REVIEW HEARING PURSUANT TO SECTION 16-8.5-112.
13 (b) IF THE COURT FINDS THE DEFENDANT IS UNRESTORABLE
14 PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (4)(a)(II) OR (4)(a)(III) OF THIS SECTION, THE
15 COURT SHALL ORDER THE DISMISSAL OF THE CRIMINAL PROCEEDINGS

20 SECTION 16-8.5-118.

4 (1) (a) [Formerly 16-8.5-113 (1)] The court may, order UPON A

10 16-8.5-108 (1)(d). FOR THE PURPOSES OF THIS SUBSECTION (1)(a), GOOD
11 CAUSE INCLUDES IF A DEFENDANT IS APPROACHING THE MAXIMUM TIME
12 PERMITTED TO RESTORE THE DEFENDANT PURSUANT TO SECTION
13 16-8.5-116. A RESTORATION HEARING MAY BE COMBINED WITH A
14 RESTORABILITY HEARING, IF APPROPRIATE.
15 (b) THE COURT SHALL SET A RESTORATION HEARING WITHIN THE

19 DEFENDANT RESTORATION SERVICES PURSUANT TO SECTION 16-8.5-110

22 THAT THE DEFENDANT IS COMPETENT TO PROCEED; AND
23 (III) A RESTORATION HEARING HAS NOT BEEN HELD OR ONE
24 HUNDRED EIGHTY-TWO DAYS HAVE PASSED AFTER A FINDING AT A
25 RESTORATION HEARING THAT THE DEFENDANT REMAINS INCOMPETENT TO

27 SERVICES.

-57- 149

1 restoration for an aggregate time of six months; and

-60- 149

1 THE COURT FINDS THERE IS AN ACCEPTABLE CARE COORDINATION

6 FOR THE DEFENDANT, INCLUDING COORDINATING WITH GOVERNMENTAL

8 PROVIDING RESOURCES TO THE DEFENDANT;

10 PSYCHOLOGICAL ASSESSMENTS OF THE DEFENDANT TO HELP DETERMINE

10 IMPOSITION OF A LEGAL DISABILITY OR REMOVAL OF A LEGAL RIGHT
11 PURSUANT TO SECTION 25.5-10-216; AND
12 (c) ONE OF THE FOLLOWING INDIVIDUALS REQUESTS THE COURT TO

14 (I) THE DISTRICT ATTORNEY;
15 (II) THE COUNTY ATTORNEY;
16 (III) THE APPOINTED EMERGENCY GUARDIAN;
17 (IV) THE PROFESSIONAL PERSON OR INTERVENING PROFESSIONAL
18 WHO OPINED PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (4)(a)(I) OF THIS SECTION THAT

20 PROFESSIONAL PERSON'S OR INTERVENING PROFESSIONAL'S OPINION IS NOT
21 THE RESULT OF WORK PERFORMED AS PART OF EMPLOYMENT OR A
22 CONTRACT WITH THE BHA OR WITH CDHS; OR
23 (V) A REPRESENTATIVE DESIGNATED BY THE BHA OR WITH CDHS
24 TO MAKE A REQUEST.
25 (7) NOTWITHSTANDING SUBSECTIONS (4) AND (6) OF THIS SECTION,
26 IF AN APPOINTED EMERGENCY GUARDIAN BELIEVES IN THEIR

1 INITIATED, THE COURT SHALL ALLOW THE EMERGENCY GUARDIAN TO

4 (8) (a) THE COURT SHALL GRANT AN UNLIMITED NUMBER OF
5 THIRTY-FIVE-DAY EXTENSIONS TO STAY THE ORDER DISMISSING THE
6 DEFENDANT'S CASE IF THE DEFENDANT CONSENTS TO THE EXTENSION.
7 (b) IF THE DEFENDANT DOES NOT CONSENT TO AN EXTENSION TO
8 STAY THE ORDER, THE COURT SHALL GRANT NO MORE THAN FOUR
9 ADDITIONAL EXTENSIONS, SO LONG AS THE TOTAL EXTENSIONS DO NOT
10 EXCEED ONE HUNDRED SEVENTY-FIVE DAYS, IF:
11 (I) THE PROSECUTING ATTORNEY REQUESTS AN EXTENSION,
12 REGARDLESS OF WHETHER THE DEFENDANT CONSENTS TO THE EXTENSION;
13 (II) THE COURT FINDS GOOD CAUSE; AND
14 (III) THE DEFENDANT IS CHARGED WITH AN ACT THAT
15 CONSTITUTES HOMICIDE PURSUANT TO PART 1 OF ARTICLE 3 OF TITLE 18;
16 A CRIME OF VIOLENCE, AS DEFINED IN SECTION 18-1.3-406 (2); OR A
17 FELONY THAT CONSTITUTES UNLAWFUL SEXUAL BEHAVIOR, AS DEFINED IN
18 SECTION 16-22-102.
19 (c) IF THE DEFENDANT DOES NOT CONSENT TO AN EXTENSION TO

22 SEVENTY DAYS, IF:
23 (I) THE PROSECUTING ATTORNEY REQUESTS AN EXTENSION,

25 (II) THE COURT FINDS GOOD CAUSE; AND
26 (III) THE DEFENDANT IS NOT CHARGED WITH A CRIME OF

-67- 149

1 CONSTITUTES UNLAWFUL SEXUAL BEHAVIOR, AS DEFINED IN SECTION
2 16-22-102.
3 (d) FOR THE PURPOSE OF THIS SUBSECTION (8), GOOD CAUSE DOES
4 NOT INCLUDE REFUSAL OR FAILURE TO TIMELY FILE A PETITION FOR A CIVIL
5 PROCEEDING PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (4) OF THIS SECTION.
6 (e) AN EXTENSION GRANTED PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (8)(b) OR
7 (8)(c) OF THIS SECTION IS IN ADDITION TO THE INITIAL STAY AUTHORIZED
8 PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (6)(a) OF THIS SECTION.

12 16-8.5-118. AFTER THE DEFENDANT'S CASE IS DISMISSED, THE COURT

14 FOR THE DISMISSAL.

27 INTENDS THE COUNTY ATTORNEY TO INITIATE; AND
-68- 149

1 (d) INCLUDES ANY ORDERS THE COURT ISSUED PURSUANT TO THIS
2 SECTION.

4 GUARDIAN MAY OBJECT TO THE ORDER TO INITIATE A CIVIL PROCEEDING

9 FORTHWITH AND RULE ON THE COUNTY ATTORNEY'S OBJECTION BY EITHER

1 (III) ORDER CDHS TO IDENTIFY AN APPROPRIATE PROVIDER AND

26 (4) (a) THE TRIAL MUST BE CIVIL IN NATURE BUT CONDUCTED IN

-71- 149

1 COURT JUDGE OR, WITH THE CONSENT OF BOTH PARTIES, A COUNTY COURT

4 (b) (I) UPON SETTING THE TRIAL, THE COURT SHALL ORDER, IN

9 (II) THE COURT MAY ORDER THE PARTIES TO COMPLY WITH

11 PROCEDURE, BUT ONLY TO THE EXTENT REASONABLE FOR THE CIVIL TRIAL

13 (c) THE PARTIES MAY ISSUE SUBPOENAS PURSUANT TO RULE 17 OF

16 (5) (a) THE DEFENDANT MAY REQUEST TO STIPULATE THAT THE

18 RATHER THAN CONTESTING THE MATTER AT A TRIAL. THE COURT SHALL
19 ALLOW THE DEFENDANT TO STIPULATE TO THE COURT ORDERING A CIVIL
20 COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT ONLY IF:
21 (I) THE COURT CONFIRMS THE DEFENDANT HAS BEEN FULLY
22 ADVISED OF THE CONSEQUENCES AND THE RIGHTS THE DEFENDANT IS
23 WAIVING, INCLUDING THE RIGHT TO A COURT TRIAL;
24 (II) THE STIPULATION IS IN WRITING AND STATED ON THE RECORD
25 AT A HEARING AT WHICH THE DEFENDANT APPEARS, WITH THE CONSENT OF
26 ANY GUARDIAN THAT HAS BEEN APPOINTED;
27 (III) THE COURT FINDS THE DEFENDANT UNDERSTANDS THE
-72- 149

1 STIPULATION AND THAT THE STIPULATION IS VOLUNTARY DESPITE THE
2 DEFENDANT BEING INCOMPETENT TO PROCEED; AND
3 (IV) THE PARTIES ESTABLISH A FACTUAL BASIS.
4 (b) ANY STIPULATION OR ADMISSION MADE AS PART OF THE
5 STIPULATION TO IMPOSE A CIVIL COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED PROTECTIVE
6 PLACEMENT IS NOT ADMISSIBLE IN ANY FUTURE CRIMINAL PROSECUTION
7 TO PROVE ANY ACT ALLEGED IN THE WRITTEN NOTICE SEEKING THE CIVIL
8 COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT. (6) AT TRIAL, THE

10 (a) THE DEFENDANT HAS A MENTAL DISABILITY OR
11 DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY;
12 (b) (I) THE DEFENDANT COMMITTED AN ACT THAT, IN THE

14 WOULD CONSTITUTE:
15 (A) HOMICIDE PURSUANT TO PART 1 OF ARTICLE 3 OF TITLE 18;
16 (B) A CRIME OF VIOLENCE, AS DEFINED IN SECTION 18-1.3-406 (2);
17 OR
18 (C) A FELONY THAT CONSTITUTES UNLAWFUL SEXUAL BEHAVIOR,
19 AS DEFINED IN SECTION 16-22-102; AND
20 (II) THE ACT DESCRIBED IN SUBSECTION (6)(b)(I) OF THIS SECTION
21 THAT THE DEFENDANT IS ALLEGED TO HAVE COMMITTED IS OR WAS
22 CHARGED IN A CRIMINAL CASE IN COLORADO IN WHICH COMPETENCY WAS
23 RAISED; AND
24 (c) THE DEFENDANT POSES A SUBSTANTIAL RISK OF SERIOUS HARM
25 TO OTHERS.
26 (7) (a) WHEN DETERMINING WHETHER THE PROSECUTION HAS MET
27 ITS BURDEN PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (6)(b) OF THIS SECTION:
-73- 149

1 (I) THE DEFENDANT IS NOT PERMITTED TO RAISE, AND THE COURT
2 SHALL NOT CONSIDER, A DEFENSE BASED ON A MENTAL DISABILITY OR
3 DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY, INCLUDING A MENTAL DISEASE OR DEFECT,
4 AS DEFINED IN SECTION 16-8-102, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, A
5 DEFENSE RAISED PURSUANT TO ARTICLE 8 OF TITLE 16 OR ANY DEFENSE
6 THAT IS AN ELEMENT-NEGATING TRAVERSE BASED ON A MENTAL DISEASE
7 OR DEFECT;
8 (II) THE DEFENDANT IS NOT PERMITTED TO RAISE, AND THE COURT
9 SHALL NOT CONSIDER, A DEFENSE OF INTOXICATION, AS DEFINED IN
10 SECTION 18-1-804, EXCEPT FOR INTOXICATION THAT IS NOT SELF-INDUCED
11 PURSUANT TO SECTION 18-1-804 (3); AND
12 (III) THE DEFENDANT IS PERMITTED TO RAISE OTHER DEFENSES
13 THAT ARE NOT BASED ON A MENTAL DISABILITY OR DEVELOPMENTAL
14 DISABILITY, INCLUDING AFFIRMATIVE DEFENSES, EXCEPT AS PROVIDED IN
15 THIS SUBSECTION (7).
16 (b) WHEN DETERMINING WHETHER TO PERMIT A DEFENDANT TO
17 RAISE A DEFENSE PURSUANT TO THIS SUBSECTION (7), THE COURT SHALL
18 CONSIDER THE NEED TO ENSURE FAIRNESS AND DUE PROCESS TO BOTH
19 PARTIES WITHOUT FRUSTRATING THE INTENDED FUNCTIONING AND
20 LIMITED PURPOSE OF THE CIVIL PROCEEDING.
21 (c) THE PROSECUTION SHALL NOT ALLEGE, AND THE COURT SHALL
22 NOT CONSIDER, AN ACT OR SERIES OF ACTS FOR WHICH THE DEFENDANT
23 WAS ACQUITTED OR CONVICTED AS THE QUALIFYING ACTS SATISFYING THE
24 CRITERIA LISTED IN SUBSECTION (6)(b) OF THIS SECTION, BUT THE COURT
25 MAY ADMIT EVIDENCE OF AN ACT OR A SERIES OF ACTS FOR WHICH THE

27 PURSUANT TO THE COLORADO RULES OF EVIDENCE AND RELEVANT TO THE
-74- 149

1 CRITERIA LISTED IN SUBSECTION (6)(a) OR (6)(c) OF THIS SECTION.
2 (d) (I) PRIOR TO TRIAL, THE COURT SHALL FIND THAT THE
3 PROSECUTION HAS MET THE REQUIREMENTS IN SUBSECTION (6)(b)(II) OF
4 THIS SECTION IF AN ACT ALLEGED TO SATISFY THE REQUIREMENT OF
5 SUBSECTION (6)(b)(I) OF THIS SECTION IS:
6 (A) CHARGED IN THE CURRENT CASE; OR

20 (8) (a) PRIOR TO TRIAL, THE COURT SHALL REQUIRE THE
21 DEFENDANT TO STATE WHETHER THE DEFENDANT CONTESTS THAT THE

23 (b) IF THE DEFENDANT DOES NOT CONTEST THAT THE DEFENDANT

27 (c) (I) IF THE DEFENDANT CONTESTS THAT THE DEFENDANT HAS A
-75- 149

1 MENTAL DISABILITY OR DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY, THE COURT SHALL
2 REVIEW THE RECORD AND DETERMINE IF THE DEFENDANT OR DEFENSE
3 COUNSEL RAISED THE ISSUE OF THE DEFENDANT BEING INCOMPETENT OR
4 FAILED TO OBJECT TO A FINDING THAT THE DEFENDANT IS INCOMPETENT.
5 (II) IF THE COURT FINDS THE DEFENDANT OR DEFENSE COUNSEL
6 RAISED THE ISSUE OF THE DEFENDANT BEING INCOMPETENT OR FAILED TO
7 OBJECT TO A FINDING THAT THE DEFENDANT IS INCOMPETENT, THE COURT
8 SHALL FIND THE DEFENDANT HAS PREVIOUSLY STIPULATED THAT THE
9 DEFENDANT HAS A MENTAL DISABILITY OR DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY.
10 IF THE COURT MAKES A FINDING, THE COURT SHALL FIND THE DEFENDANT
11 HAS STIPULATED THAT THE DEFENDANT HAS A MENTAL DISABILITY OR
12 DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY AND FIND IN FAVOR OF THE PROSECUTION AT
13 TRIAL WITH REGARD TO SUBSECTION (6)(a) OF THIS SECTION.
14 (III) IF THE COURT FINDS THAT THE PROSECUTION OR COURT
15 RAISED THE ISSUE OF COMPETENCY OVER THE DEFENDANT'S OBJECTION
16 AND THE DEFENDANT CONSISTENTLY MAINTAINED AN OBJECTION BY
17 ARGUING THE DEFENDANT IS COMPETENT TO PROCEED, THE PROSECUTION
18 HAS THE BURDEN TO PROVE BY CLEAR AND CONVINCING EVIDENCE THAT
19 THE DEFENDANT HAS A MENTAL DISABILITY OR DEVELOPMENTAL
20 DISABILITY PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (6)(a) OF THIS SECTION.

1 TO WHICH THE PRIOR FINDING WAS MADE; AND

8 (13) (a) WHEN THE COURT ORDERS A CIVIL COMMITMENT OR
9 ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT, IF CDHS PROPOSES PLACING THE

11 FURTHER COURT REVIEW, THE DEFENDANT PLACED INTO INPATIENT CARE
12 AT THE DISCRETION OF CDHS. IF CDHS HAS IDENTIFIED AN APPROPRIATE
13 PROVIDER THAT DOES NOT MEET THE DEFINITION OF INPATIENT CARE BUT
14 IS WILLING AND ABLE TO ACCEPT PLACEMENT OF THE DEFENDANT, THE
15 COURT SHALL SET A REVIEW HEARING AS SOON AS PRACTICABLE WHILE
16 ENSURING THE PARTIES, ANY APPOINTED EMERGENCY GUARDIAN, AND
17 VICTIMS ARE GIVEN SUFFICIENT NOTICE AND OPPORTUNITY TO PREPARE

19 (b) WHEN THE COURT ORDERS A CIVIL COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED

21 PROVIDER THAT IS WILLING TO ACCEPT PLACEMENT OF THE DEFENDANT,
22 THE COURT SHALL:
23 (I) SET A REVIEW HEARING WITHIN THIRTY-FIVE DAYS AFTER THE
24 ORDER FOR CIVIL COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT;
25 AND
26 (II) PRIOR TO THE REVIEW HEARING, ORDER CDHS, IN
27 CONSULTATION WITH THE BHA, TO IDENTIFY AT LEAST ONE APPROPRIATE
-79- 149

1 PROVIDER THAT IS WILLING TO IMMEDIATELY ACCEPT PLACEMENT OF THE
2 DEFENDANT.
3 (c) AT THE REVIEW HEARING, IF CDHS PROPOSES PLACING THE
4 DEFENDANT INTO INPATIENT CARE, THE COURT SHALL ORDER, WITHOUT
5 FURTHER COURT REVIEW, THE DEFENDANT PLACED INTO INPATIENT CARE
6 AT THE DISCRETION OF CDHS. IF CDHS DOES NOT PROPOSE PLACING THE
7 DEFENDANT INTO INPATIENT CARE, THE COURT SHALL REVIEW ANY
8 PLACEMENT IDENTIFIED BY CDHS TO ENSURE IT IS APPROPRIATE AND
9 SUFFICIENTLY PROTECTS VICTIMS AND THE COMMUNITY FROM THE
10 SUBSTANTIAL RISK OF SERIOUS HARM TO OTHERS POSED BY THE
11 DEFENDANT. THE COURT MAY CONTINUE THE REVIEW HEARING OR ORDER
12 AN ADDITIONAL REVIEW HEARING WITHIN THE TIME FRAME SET FORTH IN
13 SUBSECTION (11)(b) OF THIS SECTION.
14 (d) WHEN CONSIDERING THE APPROPRIATENESS OF THE
15 PLACEMENT FOR THE DEFENDANT, VICTIMS, AND THE COMMUNITY, THE
16 COURT SHALL:
17 (I) ALLOW CDHS, THE PARTIES, AND ANY APPOINTED EMERGENCY
18 GUARDIAN TO BE HEARD REGARDING THE DEFENDANT'S PLACEMENT;
19 (II) GIVE DEFERENCE TO CDHS'S RECOMMENDATION ON ANY
20 ISSUE RELATED TO THE DEFENDANT'S PRIMARY DIAGNOSIS;
21 (III) GIVE DEFERENCE TO CDHS AND THE OPINION OF A MEDICAL
22 PROFESSIONAL ON THE APPROPRIATENESS OF THE PROVIDER AND
23 PLACEMENT FOR THE DEFENDANT;
24 (IV) NOT GIVE DEFERENCE TO CDHS OR A MEDICAL
25 PROFESSIONAL AS TO WHETHER THE PLACEMENT SUFFICIENTLY PROTECTS
26 VICTIMS AND THE COMMUNITY FROM THE SUBSTANTIAL RISK OF SERIOUS
27 HARM TO OTHERS POSED BY THE DEFENDANT; AND
-80- 149

1 (V) CONSIDER THE TOTALITY OF THE CIRCUMSTANCES, INCLUDING:
2 (A) THE DEFENDANT'S STATEMENTS AND WHETHER THE
3 DEFENDANT LACKS INSIGHT INTO THE DEFENDANT'S MENTAL DISABILITY
4 OR DEVELOPMENTAL DISABILITY;
5 (B) THE DEFENDANT'S CLINICAL DIAGNOSIS AND PROGNOSIS,
6 INCLUDING ANY OPINIONS THAT THE DEFENDANT AND THE DEFENDANT'S
7 CURRENT MENTAL STATE AND BEHAVIORS POSE RISKS TO OTHERS;
8 (C) THE DEFENDANT'S REFUSAL TO VOLUNTARILY SEEK AND
9 COMPLY WITH A TREATMENT PLAN IN THE RECENT PAST, INCLUDING IN THE
10 DEFENDANT'S MOST RECENT PERIOD OF BEING IN THE COMMUNITY;
11 (D) RECENT OVERT ACTS BY THE DEFENDANT TO THREATEN,
12 CAUSE, OR ATTEMPT TO CAUSE HARM TO THE DEFENDANT'S SELF OR
13 OTHERS;
14 (E) ANY PREVIOUS PATTERNS OF DECOMPENSATION OR
15 DETERIORATION THAT RESULTED IN THE DEFENDANT'S HOSPITALIZATION,
16 ARREST, OR CERTIFICATION FOR SHORT-TERM TREATMENT AND THAT
17 DEMONSTRATE A RISK OF LIKELY FUTURE DECOMPENSATION;
18 (F) WHEN THE DEFENDANT WAS LAST OUT OF CUSTODY AND
19 WHETHER THE DEFENDANT WAS FOUND IN A CONDITION IN WHICH THE
20 DEFENDANT WAS NOT ABLE TO CARE FOR THE DEFENDANT'S OWN BASIC
21 NEEDS IN ORDER TO AVOID THE RISK OF SERIOUS PHYSICAL HARM;
22 (G) THE FREQUENCY, RECENCY, AND SEVERITY OF THE
23 CONSIDERATIONS DESCRIBED IN SUBSECTIONS (13)(d)(V)(B) TO
24 (13)(d)(V)(F) OF THIS SECTION, SUCH THAT THE CIRCUMSTANCES ARE
25 PRESENT IN A MANNER THAT REQUIRES INPATIENT TREATMENT OR THAT
26 THE CIRCUMSTANCES ARE ABSENT IN A MANNER THAT ALLOWS FOR
27 COMMUNITY-BASED PLACEMENT;
-81- 149

1 (H) WHETHER THE DEFENDANT OPPOSES THE PLACEMENT;
2 (I) WHETHER COMMUNITY-BASED PLACEMENT CAN BE
3 REASONABLY ACCOMMODATED;
4 (J) THE LIMITED RESOURCES OF THE STATE AND THE NEEDS OF
5 OTHERS; AND
6 (K) THE PLACEMENT RECOMMENDATIONS OF ANY TREATING
7 PROFESSIONALS.
8 (e) THE COURT SHALL NOT PERMIT OR ORDER A DEFENDANT TO BE
9 PLACED INTO A COMMUNITY-BASED SETTING OUTSIDE OF INPATIENT CARE
10 UNLESS:
11 (I) ANY VICTIMS HAVE BEEN NOTIFIED OF A CRITICAL STAGE, AS
12 DEFINED IN SECTION 24-4.1-302 (2)(q.3), AND GIVEN THE OPPORTUNITY TO
13 BE HEARD; AND
14 (II) THE DISTRICT ATTORNEY IN THE CASE THAT SOUGHT CIVIL
15 COMMITMENT OR ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT HAS BEEN NOTIFIED
16 AND GIVEN AN OPPORTUNITY TO OBJECT AND BE HEARD.
17 (f) AFTER REVIEWING THE PROVIDERS, IF THE COURT FINDS THAT
18 MULTIPLE PROVIDERS ARE APPROPRIATE FOR THE DEFENDANT, VICTIMS,
19 AND THE COMMUNITY, THE COURT SHALL ORDER THE DEFENDANT BE
20 PLACED IN THE LEAST-RESTRICTIVE SETTING THAT IS ADEQUATE TO
21 PROTECT THE VICTIMS AND THE COMMUNITY AND TO PROVIDE, TO THE
22 GREATEST EXTENT POSSIBLE, THE APPROPRIATE LEVEL OF CARE FOR,
23 TREATMENT FOR, AND SUPERVISION OF THE DEFENDANT. IF MORE THAN
24 ONE EQUALLY RESTRICTIVE PLACEMENT IS AVAILABLE AND APPROPRIATE,
25 THE COURT SHALL ORDER THE DEFENDANT BE PLACED INTO A SETTING
26 THAT IS BEST SUITED FOR THE DEFENDANT'S TREATMENT NEEDS AND
27 SUPERVISION, AS DETERMINED BY CDHS.
-82- 149

1 (g) IF CDHS DOES NOT PROPOSE PLACING THE DEFENDANT INTO
2 INPATIENT CARE AND CDHS HAS NOT IDENTIFIED ANY OTHER
3 APPROPRIATE PLACEMENT WITH SUFFICIENT TIME TO REVIEW AND PLACE
4 THE DEFENDANT WITHIN THE TIME FRAME SET FORTH IN SUBSECTION
5 (11)(b) OF THIS SECTION, THE COURT SHALL ORDER THE DEFENDANT
6 PLACED INTO INPATIENT CARE AT THE DISCRETION OF CDHS.
7 (h) IN ADDITION TO ANY ORDERS ISSUED PURSUANT TO THIS
8 SECTION, THE COURT SHALL ISSUE ANY ORDERS NECESSARY TO MITIGATE
9 THE DEFENDANT'S RISK TO VICTIMS AND THE COMMUNITY, INCLUDING
10 ORDERING A PROVIDER THAT HAS ACCEPTED PLACEMENT OF THE
11 DEFENDANT TO TAKE REASONABLE AND PRACTICABLE PROTECTIVE
12 MEASURES TO PREVENT THE DEFENDANT FROM CONTACTING ANY VICTIMS;
13 EXCEPT THAT THE COURT SHALL NOT ORDER THE DEFENDANT TO COMPLY
14 WITH ANY CONDITIONS THAT ARE NOT NECESSARY TO MITIGATE THE
15 DEFENDANT'S RISK OR WITH WHICH THE DEFENDANT CANNOT COMPLY.
16 (i) AFTER THE COURT ORDERS PLACEMENT OF THE DEFENDANT:
17 (I) THE COURT, THE DISTRICT ATTORNEY, AND CDHS SHALL
18 TRANSMIT ALL NECESSARY INFORMATION, INCLUDING THE DEFENDANT'S
19 MEDICAL RECORDS, COMPETENCY EVALUATIONS, MATERIALS USED DURING
20 THE COMPETENCY PROCESS, AND RESTORATION RECORDS, TO THE COUNTY
21 ATTORNEY WITHIN THREE BUSINESS DAYS AFTER THE ORDER FOR
22 PLACEMENT. THE DISTRICT ATTORNEY AND CDHS SHALL PROVIDE
23 ADDITIONAL NECESSARY INFORMATION UPON REQUEST OF THE COUNTY
24 ATTORNEY.
25 (II) THE COURT SHALL, UNLESS OTHER APPROPRIATE
26 TRANSPORTATION HAS BEEN APPROVED BY THE COURT, ORDER THE
27 SHERIFF TO SECURELY TRANSPORT THE DEFENDANT TO THE ORDERED
-83- 149

1 PLACEMENT AS SOON AS PRACTICABLE. THE COURT SHALL SET ANY
2 REVIEW HEARINGS NECESSARY TO ENSURE THE DEFENDANT IS
3 TRANSPORTED TO THE INITIAL PLACEMENT. THE COURT MAY DELAY
4 TRANSPORTATION IF A BED AT A PLACEMENT IS NOT AVAILABLE BUT WILL
5 BE AVAILABLE WITHIN THE NEXT THIRTY DAYS.
6 (14) AFTER THE DEFENDANT HAS BEEN TRANSPORTED TO THE
7 PLACEMENT ORDERED BY THE COURT, THE COURT SHALL:
8 (a) TRANSFER JURISDICTION OF THE CIVIL COMMITMENT TO A CIVIL
9 COURT WITH JURISDICTION PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-113 OR
10 TRANSFER JURISDICTION OF THE ENHANCED PROTECTIVE PLACEMENT TO
11 A CIVIL COURT WITH JURISDICTION PURSUANT TO SECTION 25.5-10-507;
12 (b) NOTIFY CDHS AND THE COUNTY ATTORNEY; AND
13 (c) DISMISS THE CRIMINAL CASE AGAINST THE DEFENDANT. THE
14 COURT SHALL NOTE IN THE COURT'S FINAL ORDER DISMISSING THE CASE
15 THAT THE CHARGES MAY BE REFILED, BUT THE PROSECUTION IS NOT
16 PRECLUDED FROM REFILING MERELY BECAUSE THE COURT DID NOT STATE
17 SO IN THE ORDER.
18 (15) SUBJECT TO THE APPLICABLE STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS,
19 INCLUDING ANY APPLICABLE TOLLING PROVISIONS, THE DISTRICT
20 ATTORNEY MAY REFILE THE CHARGES DISMISSED PURSUANT TO THIS

3 16-8.5-119. Dismissal procedures.
4 (1) IF, AFTER ALL APPLICABLE STAYS ARE LIFTED, THE COURT
5 DISMISSES A DEFENDANT'S CRIMINAL CASE IN WHICH THE DEFENDANT IS
6 INCOMPETENT TO PROCEED AT THE TIME OF DISMISSAL:
7 (a) THE COURT SHALL ENTER A WRITTEN ORDER WITH THE COURT'S
8 LEGAL AND FACTUAL BASIS FOR THE DISMISSAL AND PROVIDE THE
9 WRITTEN ORDER TO THE PARTIES AND CDHS;
10 (b) THE COURT MAY ORDER THE BRIDGES COURT LIAISON TO ASSIST
11 WITH THE DEFENDANT'S CASE MANAGEMENT PLANNING AND
12 COORDINATION OF SERVICES, INCLUDING COORDINATING WITH
13 GOVERNMENTAL ENTITIES OR COMMUNITY-BASED ORGANIZATIONS THAT
14 ARE CAPABLE OF PROVIDING RESOURCES TO THE DEFENDANT UPON
15 DISMISSAL OF CHARGES;
16 (c) THE CHARGES ARE NOT ELIGIBLE FOR SEALING PURSUANT TO
17 SECTION 24-72-705; AND
18 (d) THE COURT SHALL REQUIRE CDHS TO ENSURE THAT CASE
19 MANAGEMENT SERVICES AND SUPPORTS ARE MADE AVAILABLE TO A
20 DEFENDANT RELEASED FROM CDHS'S CUSTODY PURSUANT TO THIS
21 ARTICLE 8.5 AFTER THE DEFENDANT'S CRIMINAL CASE IS DISMISSED.
22 (2) SUBJECT TO THE APPLICABLE STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS,
23 INCLUDING ANY APPLICABLE TOLLING PROVISIONS, THE DISTRICT
24 ATTORNEY MAY REFILE THE CHARGES DISMISSED PURSUANT TO THIS
25 SECTION AT A LATER TIME IF THE DISTRICT ATTORNEY HAS REASON TO
26 BELIEVE THE DEFENDANT HAS SINCE ATTAINED COMPETENCY. THE COURT
27 SHALL NOTE IN THE COURT'S FINAL ORDER DISMISSING THE CASE THAT THE
-87- 149

1 CHARGES MAY BE REFILED, BUT THE PROSECUTION IS NOT PRECLUDED

3 ORDER.
4 16-8.5-120. [Formerly 16-8.5-117] Escape - return to
5 institution.
6 If a defendant committed to the custody of the executive director
7 for a competency evaluation or for restoration to competency escapes
8 from the institution or hospital, the chief officer of the institution or
9 hospital shall apply to the district court for the county in which the
10 institution or hospital is located for a warrant of arrest directed to the
11 sheriff of the county, commanding the sheriff to take all necessary legal
12 action to effect the arrest of the defendant and to return the defendant
13 promptly to the institution or hospital. The fact of an escape becomes a
14 part of the official record of the defendant and must be certified to the
15 committing court as part of the record in any proceeding to determine
16 whether the defendant is eligible for release on bond or from custody.
17 16-8.5-121. [Formerly 16-8.5-118] Temporary removal for
18 treatment and rehabilitation.
19 The chief officer of an institution where a defendant has been
20 committed pursuant to this article 8.5 may authorize treatment and
21 rehabilitation activities involving temporary physical removal of the
22 defendant from the institution where the defendant has been placed in
23 accordance with the procedures and requirements of section 16-8-118.
24 16-8.5-122. [Formerly 16-8.5-120] Competency evaluation
25 monitoring system - users - rules.
26 (1) The department CDHS, with assistance from the judicial
27 department, shall develop an electronic system to track the status of
-88- 149

1 defendants in the criminal justice system for whom a competency
2 evaluation or competency restoration has been ordered. The system must
3 contain information on the following:
4 (a) The date the court ordered the COMPETENCY evaluation;
5 (b) The dates of and locations where the COMPETENCY evaluation
6 was started and completed;
7 (c) The date of and location where the defendant entered
8 restoration services;
9 (d) The dates and results of court reviews of competency;
10 (e) Inpatient bed space;
11 (f) Community restoration capacity; and
12 (g) Financial estimates of costs of each inpatient and outpatient
13 program to identify inefficiencies.
14 (2) The department CDHS shall establish who has access to enter
15 information into the electronic system and who may have read-only
16 access to the electronic system.
17 16-8.5-123. [Formerly 16-8.5-122] Forensic evaluator training.
18 By February 1, 2020, the department CDHS shall create a
19 partnership with an accredited institution of higher education in the state
20 to develop and provide rigorous training in forensic evaluation. On or
21 before January 1, 2021, Newly hired competency evaluators must SHALL
22 complete a training that addresses competency, sanity, report writing,
23 expert testimony, and other skills crucial for forensic evaluators; except
24 that competency evaluators who are forensic psychiatrists certified or
25 certification-eligible by the American board of psychiatry and neurology
26 and forensic psychologists who are certified or certification-eligible by
27 the American board of forensic psychology may be exempt from any
-89- 149

1 training requirements as outlined in this section through an exemption
2 process to be developed by the department CDHS. The state will SHALL
3 manage an oversight program that will TO provide support and ensure
4 quality of forensic evaluators.
5 16-8.5-124. Transparency requirements.
6 (1) The department CDHS shall post publicly POST on the office
7 of civil and forensic mental health's CDHS'S website:
8 (a) All policies and procedures related to competency evaluations,
9 restoration services, management of the competency wait list, and
10 admission policies regarding inpatient restoration services, including
11 services for jail-based restoration and private hospital beds;
12 (b) The number of beds currently available and occupied for
13 jail-based restoration services;
14 (c) The number of beds currently available and occupied in private
15 hospitals for inpatient restoration services;
16 (d) The number of beds currently available in each state-run
17 hospital and occupied by adult civil patients, adult restoration patients,
18 and adult not guilty by reason of insanity commitments;
19 (e) The number of beds currently available in each state-run
20 hospital and occupied by juvenile patients;
21 (f) The number of individuals on the competency restoration wait
22 list;
23 (g) The length of competency wait list times and an explanation
24 of the methodology used to calculate the wait times; and
25 (h) Any projected dates for the opening of new beds and a
26 description of what type of beds will become available.

20 As used in this article 65, unless the context otherwise requires:

23 27-65-106. Emergency mental health hold - screening -

26 (1) An emergency mental health hold may be invoked INITIATED
27 under one of the following conditions:
-94- 149

1 (a) (I) When a certified peace officer has probable cause to
2 believe a person has a mental health disorder and, as a result of the mental
3 health disorder, is an imminent danger to the person's self, or AN
4 IMMINENT DANGER TO others, or is gravely disabled, the certified peace
5 officer may take the person into protective custody and transport the
6 person to a facility designated by the commissioner for an emergency
7 mental health hold. If such a facility is not available, the certified peace
8 officer may transport the person to an emergency medical services

1 the respondent's condition and alleging that the respondent HAS A

6 (2) (a) When a person is taken into custody pursuant to subsection

18 27-65-108.3. Criteria and standards for certification for
19 short-term treatment and certification for long-term care and
20 treatment.
21 (1) A RESPONDENT MAY BE CERTIFIED FOR SHORT-TERM
22 TREATMENT PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-108.5, 27-65-109, OR
23 27-65-109.5, OR CERTIFIED FOR LONG-TERM CARE AND TREATMENT
24 PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-110, IF:
25 (a) THE RESPONDENT HAS BEEN ADVISED OF THE AVAILABILITY OF,
26 BUT HAS NOT ACCEPTED, VOLUNTARY TREATMENT;
27 (b) WITH THE CONSIDERATION OF ALL REASONABLY AVAILABLE
-103- 149

1 INFORMATION, INCLUDING THE RELEVANT HISTORY OF THE RESPONDENT,
2 THERE ARE REASONABLE GROUNDS TO BELIEVE THAT THE RESPONDENT
3 WILL NOT REMAIN IN A VOLUNTARY TREATMENT PROGRAM;
4 (c) THE RESPONDENT, BY CLEAR AND CONVINCING EVIDENCE, HAS
5 A MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER AND, AS A RESULT OF THE MENTAL HEALTH
6 DISORDER, THE RESPONDENT IS A DANGER TO THE RESPONDENT'S SELF, A
7 DANGER TO OTHERS, OR GRAVELY DISABLED; OR
8 (d) THE RESPONDENT, BY CLEAR AND CONVINCING EVIDENCE, HAS
9 A PERSISTENT MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER.
10 (2) WHEN EVALUATING A PERSON TO DETERMINE WHETHER THE
11 PERSON MEETS THE CRITERIA PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (1) OF THIS
12 SECTION, THE COURT, EVALUATOR, OR INTERVENING PROFESSIONAL SHALL
13 TAKE INTO CONSIDERATION:
14 (a) THE PERSON'S STATEMENTS AND INSIGHTS INTO THE PERSON'S
15 OWN MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER;
16 (b) CLINICAL DIAGNOSIS AND CLINICAL PERSPECTIVE ON THE
17 PERSON'S CURRENT MENTAL STATE AND PROGNOSIS;
18 (c) THE PERSON'S WILLINGNESS TO VOLUNTARILY SEEK AND
19 COMPLY WITH A TREATMENT PLAN IN THE REASONABLY FORESEEABLE
20 FUTURE;
21 (d) RECENT OVERT ACTS BY THE PERSON TO THREATEN, CAUSE, OR
22 ATTEMPT TO CAUSE HARM TO THE PERSON'S SELF OR OTHERS;
23 (e) PREVIOUS PATTERNS OF DETERIORATION THAT RESULTED IN
24 THE PERSON'S HOSPITALIZATION, ARREST, OR CERTIFICATION FOR
25 SHORT-TERM TREATMENT;
26 (f) WHETHER THE PERSON WAS FOUND IN A CONDITION WHERE THE
27 PERSON WAS NOT ABLE TO CARE FOR THE PERSON'S OWN BASIC NEEDS IN
-104- 149

1 ORDER TO AVOID THE RISK OF SERIOUS PHYSICAL HARM; AND
2 (g) THE FREQUENCY, RECENCY, AND SEVERITY OF THE
3 CONSIDERATIONS DESCRIBED IN SUBSECTIONS (2)(b) TO (2)(f) OF THIS
4 SECTION AND THE LIKELIHOOD THAT THE CONDITIONS AND EVENTS WILL
5 REOCCUR WITHOUT INVOLUNTARY TREATMENT.
6 (3) WHEN EVALUATING WHETHER A PERSON IS A DANGER TO THE
7 PERSON'S SELF OR A DANGER TO OTHERS, IS GRAVELY DISABLED, HAS A
8 PERSISTENT MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER, OR POSES A SUBSTANTIAL RISK OF
9 SERIOUS HARM TO OTHERS, THE COURT, EVALUATOR, OR INTERVENING
10 PROFESSIONAL SHALL, WHENEVER POSSIBLE, USE ALL REASONABLE
11 EFFORTS TO LEARN ABOUT PRIOR RELEVANT BEHAVIORS AND PRIOR
12 DIAGNOSES THROUGH AVAILABLE AND RELIABLE SOURCES, INCLUDING THE
13 PERSON'S PRIOR MEDICAL AND MENTAL HEALTH RECORDS, POLICE
14 REPORTS, AND INFORMATION FROM RELIABLE INDIVIDUALS WHO HAVE A
15 RELATIONSHIP OR REGULAR SUBSTANTIAL INTERACTIONS WITH THE
16 PERSON.
17 (4) THE COURT OR PROFESSIONAL PERSON SHALL ASSESS A PERSON
18 WHO IS INCARCERATED OR IN INPATIENT TREATMENT AS IF THE PERSON
19 WERE IN THE COMMUNITY WHEN EVALUATING WHETHER THE PERSON
20 MEETS THE CRITERIA PURSUANT TO SUBSECTION (1) OF THIS SECTION.

9 PETITIONER.
10 (b) PRIOR TO FILING THE PETITION, THE PETITIONER SHALL:
11 (I) ASK THE RESPONDENT TO DESIGNATE A LAY PERSON WHOM THE
12 RESPONDENT WISHES TO BE INFORMED REGARDING THE OUTPATIENT
13 CERTIFICATION;
14 (II) PROVIDE THE RESPONDENT WITH A COPY OF THE OUTPATIENT
15 CERTIFICATION; AND
16 (III) PROVIDE THE RESPONDENT WITH THE CONTACT INFORMATION
17 FOR THE COURT IN WHICH THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION WILL BE FILED
18 AND FOR ANY DESIGNATED PROVIDER THAT IS WILLING TO HOLD THE
19 OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION AND THAT HAS BEEN IDENTIFIED BY THE
20 COMMISSIONER TO PROVIDE TREATMENT.
21 (3) THE SIGNED OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION MUST:
22 (a) STATE SUFFICIENT FACTS TO ESTABLISH REASONABLE GROUNDS
23 THAT THE RESPONDENT MEETS THE CRITERIA SET FORTH IN SECTION
24 27-65-108.3;
25 (b) BE FILED WITH THE COURT WITHIN FOURTEEN DAYS,
26 EXCLUDING SATURDAYS, SUNDAYS, AND COURT HOLIDAYS, AFTER THE
27 INITIATING PARTY RECEIVED THE COURT ORDER FROM THE CRIMINAL
-113- 149

1 COURT INITIATING THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION, AND A COPY MUST BE
2 PROVIDED TO CDHS AND THE BHA WITHIN TWENTY-FOUR HOURS AFTER
3 FILING THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION;
4 (c) PROVIDE ALL CONTACT INFORMATION THAT THE PETITIONER
5 HAS FOR THE RESPONDENT;
6 (d) PROVIDE THE NAME AND CONTACT INFORMATION FOR THE LAY
7 PERSON DESIGNATED BY THE RESPONDENT, OR FOR ANY FAMILY OR
8 FRIENDS OF THE RESPONDENT IF THE RESPONDENT WAS UNWILLING OR
9 INCAPABLE OF DESIGNATING A LAY PERSON; AND
10 (e) IDENTIFY ANY DESIGNATED PROVIDER THAT IS WILLING TO
11 HOLD THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION AND THAT HAS BEEN IDENTIFIED BY
12 THE COMMISSIONER TO PROVIDE TREATMENT, OR INCLUDE A STATEMENT
13 THAT A DESIGNATED PROVIDER NEEDS TO BE IDENTIFIED.
14 (4) (a) ONCE THE BHA RECEIVES THE NOTICE OF OUTPATIENT
15 CERTIFICATION, IF NO DESIGNATED PROVIDER HAS BEEN IDENTIFIED, THE
16 BHA SHALL PROVIDE CARE COORDINATION PURSUANT TO SECTION
17 27-65-108, WHICH INCLUDES MAKING DILIGENT EFFORTS TO IDENTIFY A
18 DESIGNATED PROVIDER TO HOLD THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION AND
19 PROVIDE CARE TO THE RESPONDENT. THE BHA SHALL KEEP THE COURT,
20 PETITIONER, AND COUNTY ATTORNEY INFORMED IN WRITING REGARDING
21 ANY DESIGNATED PROVIDER THAT WILL HOLD THE OUTPATIENT
22 CERTIFICATION AND PROVIDE CARE TO THE RESPONDENT.
23 (b) ONCE A PROVIDER IS DESIGNATED TO HOLD THE OUTPATIENT
24 CERTIFICATION, THE PROVIDER SHALL NOTIFY THE BHA IF THE PROVIDER
25 HAS NOT MADE CONTACT WITH THE RESPONDENT WITHIN SEVEN DAYS
26 AFTER ACCEPTING THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION. THE BHA SHALL
27 NOTIFY THE COURT IN WRITING THAT THE PROVIDER HAS NOT MADE
-114- 149

1 CONTACT WITH THE RESPONDENT WITHIN SEVEN DAYS AFTER ACCEPTING
2 THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION.
3 (5) IF THE RESPONDENT HAS NOT DESIGNATED A LAY PERSON
4 WHOM THE RESPONDENT WISHES TO BE INFORMED REGARDING THE
5 OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION, THE RESPONDENT MUST BE ASKED AND
6 ALLOWED TO DESIGNATE A LAY PERSON AS SOON AS THE RESPONDENT IS
7 CAPABLE AND WILLING TO DO SO.
8 (6) WHENEVER AN OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION IS FILED WITH THE
9 COURT BY A PROFESSIONAL PERSON OR INTERVENING PROFESSIONAL, THE
10 COURT SHALL IMMEDIATELY APPOINT AN ATTORNEY TO REPRESENT THE
11 RESPONDENT. THE RESPONDENT HAS THE RIGHT TO AN ATTORNEY FOR ALL
12 PROCEEDINGS CONDUCTED PURSUANT TO THIS SECTION, INCLUDING ANY
13 APPEALS. THE ATTORNEY REPRESENTING THE RESPONDENT MUST BE
14 PROVIDED WITH A COPY OF THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION AND ALL
15 SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION IMMEDIATELY UPON THE ATTORNEY'S
16 APPOINTMENT. THE RESPONDENT MAY ONLY WAIVE COUNSEL WHEN THE
17 RESPONDENT MAKES A KNOWING AND INTELLIGENT WAIVER IN FRONT OF
18 THE COURT.
19 (7) THE RESPONDENT OR THE RESPONDENT'S ATTORNEY MAY AT
20 ANY TIME FILE A WRITTEN REQUEST THAT THE CERTIFICATION FOR
21 SHORT-TERM TREATMENT OR THE TREATMENT BE REVIEWED BY THE
22 COURT. IF A REVIEW IS REQUESTED, THE COURT SHALL HEAR THE MATTER
23 WITHIN TEN DAYS AFTER THE REQUEST, AND THE COURT SHALL GIVE
24 NOTICE TO THE RESPONDENT AND THE RESPONDENT'S ATTORNEY AND THE
25 CERTIFYING AND TREATING PROFESSIONAL PERSON OR INTERVENING
26 PROFESSIONAL OF THE TIME AND PLACE OF THE HEARING. THE HEARING
27 MUST BE HELD IN ACCORDANCE WITH SECTION 27-65-113.1. AT THE
-115- 149

1 CONCLUSION OF THE HEARING, THE COURT MAY ENTER OR CONFIRM THE
2 OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION FOR SHORT-TERM TREATMENT, DISCHARGE
3 THE RESPONDENT, OR ENTER ANY OTHER APPROPRIATE ORDER.
4 (8) THIS SECTION DOES NOT REQUIRE A COURT APPEARANCE IF THE
5 RESPONDENT DOES NOT CONTEST THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION AND A
6 DESIGNATED PROVIDER IS IDENTIFIED TO HOLD THE OUTPATIENT
7 CERTIFICATION AND THE DESIGNATED PROVIDER MAKES CONTACT WITH
8 THE RESPONDENT WITHIN SEVEN DAYS AFTER THE DESIGNATED PROVIDER
9 BEGINS HOLDING THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION.
10 (9) THE COURT SHALL SET A HEARING WITHIN THIRTY DAYS IF:
11 (a) THE RESPONDENT REQUESTS TO CONTEST, MODIFY, OR
12 TERMINATE THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION;
13 (b) THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION WAS FILED AND A
14 DESIGNATED PROVIDER WAS NOT IDENTIFIED WITHIN SEVEN DAYS AFTER
15 THE FILING OF THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION; OR
16 (c) THE DESIGNATED PROVIDER DID NOT MAKE CONTACT WITH THE
17 RESPONDENT WITHIN SEVEN DAYS AFTER THE DESIGNATED PROVIDER
18 BEGAN HOLDING THE OUTPATIENT CERTIFICATION.
19 (10) THE COURT MAY SET A COURT REVIEW OR HEARING FOR GOOD
20 CAUSE SHOWN AT ANY TIME UPON THE REQUEST OF A PARTY, THE COUNTY
21 ATTORNEY RESPONSIBLE FOR PROCEEDINGS, OR THE COURT'S OWN
22 MOTION.
23 (11) THE BHA MAY RECEIVE AND POSSESS ALL INFORMATION
24 RELEVANT TO THE PROCEEDINGS PURSUANT TO THIS SECTION, INCLUDING
25 COMPETENCY AND MENTAL HEALTH EVALUATIONS; ANY MEDICAL AND
26 MENTAL HEALTH RECORDS FOR WHICH A WAIVER OR PRIVILEGE HAS BEEN
27 FOUND IN PROCEEDINGS PURSUANT TO THIS SECTION OR PURSUANT TO
-116- 149

1 ARTICLE 8 OR 8.5 OF TITLE 16; AND RELEVANT CRIMINAL JUSTICE
2 RECORDS, INCLUDING ANY CRIMINAL HISTORY OF THE RESPONDENT. THE
3 BHA MAY SHARE AND DISCUSS THE RELEVANT INFORMATION WITH THE
4 PARTIES TO THE PROCEEDINGS.
5 (12) IN ACCORDANCE WITH THE PROCEDURES DESCRIBED IN
6 SECTION 27-65-112, A RESPONDENT CERTIFIED FOR SHORT-TERM
7 TREATMENT ON AN OUTPATIENT BASIS MAY BE DISCHARGED UPON THE
8 SIGNATURE OF THE APPROVED PROFESSIONAL PERSON OVERSEEING THE
9 RESPONDENT'S TREATMENT, AND THE PROFESSIONAL PERSON SHALL
10 NOTIFY THE BHA PRIOR TO THE DISCHARGE. A FACILITY OR PROGRAM
11 SHALL MAKE THE RESPONDENT'S DISCHARGE INSTRUCTIONS AVAILABLE TO
12 THE RESPONDENT, THE RESPONDENT'S ATTORNEY, AND THE RESPONDENT'S
13 LEGAL GUARDIAN, IF APPLICABLE, WITHIN SEVEN DAYS AFTER DISCHARGE,
14 IF REQUESTED. A FACILITY OR PROGRAM THAT IS TRANSFERRING A
15 RESPONDENT TO A DIFFERENT TREATMENT FACILITY OR TO AN OUTPATIENT
16 PROVIDER SHALL PROVIDE ALL TREATMENT RECORDS TO THE FACILITY OR
17 PROVIDER ACCEPTING THE RESPONDENT AT LEAST TWENTY-FOUR HOURS
18 PRIOR TO THE TRANSFER.
19 (13) IF THE PROFESSIONAL PERSON IN CHARGE OF THE EVALUATION
20 AND TREATMENT BELIEVES THAT A PERIOD LONGER THAN THREE MONTHS
21 IS NECESSARY TO TREAT THE RESPONDENT, THE PROFESSIONAL PERSON
22 SHALL FILE WITH THE COURT AN EXTENDED CERTIFICATION AT LEAST
23 THIRTY DAYS PRIOR TO THE EXPIRATION DATE OF THE ORIGINAL
24 CERTIFICATION. AN EXTENDED CERTIFICATION FOR TREATMENT MUST NOT
25 BE FOR A PERIOD OF MORE THAN THREE MONTHS. THE RESPONDENT IS
26 ENTITLED TO A HEARING ON THE EXTENDED CERTIFICATION UNDER THE
27 SAME CONDITIONS AS AN ORIGINAL CERTIFICATION. THE ATTORNEY
-117- 149

1 INITIALLY REPRESENTING THE RESPONDENT SHALL CONTINUE TO
2 REPRESENT THE RESPONDENT, UNLESS THE COURT APPOINTS ANOTHER
3 ATTORNEY.
4 (14) THIS SECTION DOES NOT PRECLUDE ANY PROCEEDINGS OR
5 ACTIONS PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-106, 27-65-108.5, OR 27-65-109.

9 27-65-113.1. Hearing procedures.
10 (1) A HEARING HELD PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-201,

-128- 149

1 GUARDIAN CONCERNING THE APPROPRIATE DISPOSITION OF THE CASE.
2 (4) A LAY PERSON MAY SUBMIT AN AFFIDAVIT TO THE COURT
3 CONCERNING THE LAY PERSON'S RELATIONSHIP TO THE RESPONDENT, HOW
4 LONG THE LAY PERSON HAS KNOWN THE RESPONDENT, THE LAY PERSON'S
5 PHYSICAL ADDRESS, AND THE LAY PERSON'S VIEWS CONCERNING THE
6 APPROPRIATE DISPOSITION OF THE RESPONDENT'S CASE.
7 27-65-113.5. County attorney and district attorney
8 responsibilities.
9 (1) THE COUNTY ATTORNEY OR DISTRICT ATTORNEY IN A COUNTY
10 OR CITY AND COUNTY THAT HAS A POPULATION EQUAL TO OR LESS THAN
11 FIFTY THOUSAND PEOPLE, OR A QUALIFIED ATTORNEY ACTING AS THE
12 COUNTY OR DISTRICT ATTORNEY'S DESIGNEE WHO IS APPOINTED BY THE
13 DISTRICT COURT, HAS THE FOLLOWING POWERS AND RESPONSIBILITIES:
14 (a) TO FILE AND APPEAR ON BEHALF OF THE COUNTY OR STATE IN
15 ALL PROCEEDINGS BROUGHT PURSUANT TO THIS ARTICLE 65 OR
16 TRANSFERRED FROM THE CRIMINAL COURT PURSUANT TO SECTION
17 16-8.5-118, UNLESS EXPRESSLY RELIEVED OR MODIFIED BY STATUTE;
18 (b) TO ASSIST A NONPROFESSIONAL INDIVIDUAL WHO IS
19 ATTEMPTING TO INITIATE A REQUEST TO THE COURT FOR AN EVALUATION,
20 PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-106 (1)(b), OF A PERSON WHOM THE
21 NONPROFESSIONAL INDIVIDUAL BELIEVES MEETS THE CRITERIA FOR A
22 CERTIFICATION BY PROVIDING INFORMATION AND ASSISTING IN MAKING
23 FILINGS TO THE COURT;
24 (c) TO EXERCISE DUE DILIGENCE IN GATHERING INFORMATION
25 FROM AVAILABLE SOURCES FOR USE IN PROCEEDINGS BROUGHT PURSUANT
26 TO THIS ARTICLE 65; AND
27 (d) TO SHARE AND PROVIDE INFORMATION ABOUT PROCEEDINGS
-129- 149

1 BROUGHT PURSUANT TO THIS ARTICLE 65 TO INTERESTED PARTIES AS
2 ALLOWABLE BY LAW.

5 27-65-114. Appeals.
6 (1) Appellate review of any order of FOR CERTIFICATION FOR
7 short-term treatment or long-term care and treatment OR FOR CIVIL
8 COMMITMENT may be had as provided in the Colorado appellate rules. An
9 appeal must be advanced upon the calendar of the appellate court and
10 must be decided at the earliest practicable time. Pending disposition by
11 the appellate court, the court may make such order as the court may
12 consider proper in the premises relating to the care and custody of the
13 respondent.
14 (2) THE COURT SHALL ADVISE A RESPONDENT SUBJECT TO AN
15 ORDER FOR CERTIFICATION FOR SHORT-TERM TREATMENT OR LONG-TERM
16 CARE AND TREATMENT OR FOR CIVIL COMMITMENT OF THE RESPONDENT'S
17 RIGHT TO APPEAL THE ORDER AT THE CONCLUSION OF ANY HEARING.

10 27-65-201. Court supervision of incompetent and unrestorable
11 person ordered into civil commitment - repeal.
12 (1) ONCE A CRIMINAL COURT HAS TRANSFERRED JURISDICTION OF
13 A CIVIL COMMITMENT PURSUANT TO SECTION 16-8.5-118 TO A CIVIL COURT
14 WITH JURISDICTION PURSUANT TO SECTION 27-65-113, THE CIVIL COURT
15 HAS EXCLUSIVE JURISDICTION OVER THE CIVIL COMMITMENT.
16 (2) UPON RECEIVING JURISDICTION OF A CIVIL COMMITMENT, THE
17 CIVIL COURT SHALL:
18 (a) NOTIFY THE COUNTY ATTORNEY;
19 (b) APPOINT AN ATTORNEY TO REPRESENT THE RESPONDENT AND
20 PROVIDE COPIES OF ANY DOCUMENTS SENT FROM THE CRIMINAL COURT TO
21 THE RESPONDENT'S ATTORNEY; AND
22 (c) SET A REVIEW HEARING AND ORDER THE RESPONDENT
23 BROUGHT BEFORE THE COURT.
24 (3) AT THE REVIEW HEARING, THE COURT SHALL:
25 (a) ENSURE THE RESPONDENT IS REPRESENTED BY COUNSEL; AND
26 (b) ADVISE THE RESPONDENT OF THE FOLLOWING RIGHTS:
27 (I) THE RIGHT TO APPEAR IN PERSON AT ANY PROCEEDING, UNLESS
-136- 149

1 WAIVED BY THE RESPONDENT;
2 (II) THE RIGHT TO BE REPRESENTED BY PRIVATELY RETAINED
3 COUNSEL, OR COURT-APPOINTED COUNSEL IF THE RESPONDENT DOES NOT
4 HAVE PRIVATELY RETAINED COUNSEL, DURING ANY PROCEEDING HELD
5 PURSUANT TO THIS ARTICLE 65, INCLUDING ANY APPEALS;
6 (III) THE RIGHT TO REQUEST MODIFICATION OF THE TERMS OF THE
7 CIVIL COMMITMENT; AND
8 (IV) THE RIGHT TO PERIODIC REVIEW OF THE CIVIL COMMITMENT
9 AND THE RIGHT TO CONTEST, INCLUDING BY TRIAL, WHETHER THE
10 RESPONDENT QUALIFIES FOR TERMINATION OF CIVIL COMMITMENT.
11 (4) AT ANY TIME DURING THE CIVIL COMMITMENT, THE COURT
12 MAY:
13 (a) MODIFY ANY COURT ORDER OR ANY TERM OF THE CIVIL
14 COMMITMENT UPON REQUEST OF THE PARTIES AFTER GIVING THE PARTIES
15 AN OPPORTUNITY TO OBJECT AND BE HEARD;
16 (b) ISSUE A WARRANT FOR THE RESPONDENT'S ARREST AND ORDER
17 THE RESPONDENT BE BROUGHT BEFORE THE COURT IF THE COURT
18 DETERMINES THE RESPONDENT HAS FAILED TO COMPLY WITH ANY OF THE
19 COURT'S ORDERS, INCLUDING THE RESPONDENT'S UNAUTHORIZED
20 DEPARTURE FROM THE PHYSICAL CUSTODY OF A PROVIDER;

22 (I) AN UPDATED OPINION ON WHETHER THE RESPONDENT MEETS

-137- 149

1 WITHOUT HAVING ANY OTHER MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER THAT IS NOT AN

-138- 149

1 RESPONDENT.

3 THE DISCRETION OF CDHS OR IF CDHS PROPOSES TO MOVE THE
4 RESPONDENT INTO OR OUT OF INPATIENT CARE, THE COURT SHALL, PRIOR
5 TO MODIFYING THE CIVIL COMMITMENT TO CHANGE THE RESPONDENT'S

8 FITS THE RESPONDENT'S DIAGNOSIS AND TREATMENT NEEDS AND WHETHER

13 PARTIES, OR THE APPOINTED LEGAL GUARDIAN THE OPPORTUNITY TO
14 OBJECT AND BE HEARD PRIOR TO THE COURT'S DECISION. THE COURT MAY
15 PERMIT TESTIMONY IF THE COURT BELIEVES IT WOULD BE HELPFUL TO THE
16 COURT'S DECISION.
17 (d) WHEN DECIDING WHETHER TO APPROVE THE NEW PROVIDER OR
18 PLACEMENT, THE COURT SHALL GIVE DUE DEFERENCE TO CDHS AND THE
19 OPINION OF ANY MEDICAL PROFESSIONAL ON THE APPROPRIATENESS OF
20 THE PROVIDER AND PLACEMENT FOR THE RESPONDENT, VICTIMS, AND
21 COMMUNITY, BUT DEFERENCE MUST NOT BE GIVEN TO CDHS OR A
22 MEDICAL PROFESSIONAL AS TO WHETHER THE PLACEMENT SUFFICIENTLY
23 PROTECTS ANY VICTIMS AND THE COMMUNITY FROM THE SUBSTANTIAL
24 RISK OF SERIOUS HARM TO OTHERS POSED BY THE RESPONDENT. WHEN
25 CONSIDERING THE APPROPRIATENESS OF THE PLACEMENT FOR THE
26 RESPONDENT, VICTIMS, AND COMMUNITY, THE COURT SHALL CONSIDER
27 THE TOTALITY OF THE CIRCUMSTANCES, INCLUDING:
-139- 149

1 (I) THE RESPONDENT'S STATEMENTS AND INSIGHTS INTO THE
2 RESPONDENT'S MENTAL HEALTH DISORDER;
3 (II) THE CLINICAL DIAGNOSIS AND CLINICAL PERSPECTIVE ON THE

5 (III) THE RESPONDENT'S WILLINGNESS AND DEMONSTRATED
6 ABILITY TO VOLUNTARILY SEEK AND COMPLY WITH A TREATMENT PLAN IN

8 (IV) RECENT OVERT ACTS BY THE RESPONDENT TO THREATEN,

10 OTHERS;
11 (V) PREVIOUS PATTERNS OF DETERIORATION THAT RESULTED IN

14 (VI) WHETHER THE RESPONDENT WAS FOUND IN A CONDITION
